# CLOSING PASS 7 -- `crimewidthkill` (#529): VERIFY the accept-side enumeration.  Liveness token `CRIME_CLOSE7_20260915`.

VERDICT LINE FIRST: `CLOSES` (no floor gap remains in the 157-row test) or `GAP: <exact source edit to dev_sgi_ip32.c>`
or `ENUMERATION WRONG: <the cell>`, then the token.

This is a routine software-testing review of an open-source hardware emulator (GXemul) and its regression test.
"Mutant" means a deliberately modified copy of the emulator's C source used to check that the test notices the change
(standard mutation testing).  Please check the premises, not only the reasoning, and say what you could not check.
Tree: `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\` only.  File-reading seats: `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c`
(`DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`), `regress/crime_width_probe.py`, `regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/`.

## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO

Closing pass 6 found the two rows the 46-entry `ACCEPTS' list feeds (`rearm once', `latch x5') blind to the ACCEPT side of
those same cells: they COUNT notices, so an accept guard grown by one conjunct -- a cell wrongly declined -- takes the
session's one notice itself and the count stays 1 (codex's C1 and the Opus seat's G1-G5, each 141/141, four of them
guest-visible).  The flagship ruling (part A below) graded it a FLOOR breach and a CLASS, and fixed repair 12: codex's
`latch said' row (the announced form, at every ACCEPTS cell by construction -- all 46 accesses precede latch x5's lh
+0x06); item 6 narrowed to the guard-growth form with the SILENT bypass disclosed by class; value rows at the twelve cells
where a silent bypass is guest-visible, derived per cell; `free revP said'; wording.  **This pass VERIFIES the accept-side
enumeration -- every ACCEPTS cell x {announced (guard growth), silent (bypass)} -- and the generality argument for `latch
said'.  It hunts nothing else, and does not reopen the landing, lane or re-map classes that earlier passes closed.**

## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)

* **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of eight passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
  device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
  tail, which restores HARDINT, recomputes INTSTAT (it does NOT call crime_interrupt_reassert, so the line is untouched),
  answers a read 0 and returns 1.
* **History**: 40 -> ... -> 141 -> 157 rows; TEN mutation-test tables, each with a written PRETABLE and a mechanical
  prediction checker.  (The sixth-pass brief called the Opus fifth-closing seat's mutants "P1-P7"; table 9 ran four.)
* **ACCEPTS** = 46 ENTRIES: the 40-cell pre-set/re-arm floor + 2 ENDIANESS strengthenings + 4 positional repeats.
* **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).
* **TIME** updates add 66 x elapsed microseconds -- always EVEN -- so an odd seed stays odd.

## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
{
	/*
	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
	 *  physical 0x14000000.
	 *
	 *  On my real O2:
	 *
	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *
	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
	 *  would work too.)
	 *
	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
	 */
	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
	size_t i;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  Set crime version/revision:
	 *
	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
	 *
	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
	 */
	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;

	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	/*
	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
	 *
	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
	 *  bank #0."
	 */
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int mb_per_bank;
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
		mb_per_bank = 32;
	else
		mb_per_bank = 128;

	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
	size_t total_mb = 0;
	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
		else
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;

		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
	}
	
	if (len == 8) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
		else
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
	} else if (len == 4) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			if (relative_addr & 4) {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
			} else {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
			}
		} else {
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
			if (relative_addr & 4)
				odata = (int32_t)odata;
			else
				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
		}
	} else {
		/*
		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
		 *
		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
		 */
		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
		}
		goto crime_declined;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

 crime_declined:
	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
	    and is still answered.  */
	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```

+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x

+x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x18, 0x10000000); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x18, "s2"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x38, 0x11111111); c += x
+x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s3"); c += x
+x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x40, 0); c += x

## THE FLAGSHIP RULING, PART A (closing pass 6)

## PART A -- #529 crimewidthkill, closing pass 6, repair 11, 141 rows

### R1 -- A1 is a FLOOR breach, L2, and it is a CLASS.  Verified against the source, not taken from the seat.

The width arm (`dev_sgi_ip32.c:275-296`) branches on `len == 8`, `len == 4`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr & 4`; every
accepted access then reaches `switch (relative_addr)` (`:322`) and `d->reg[relative_addr / 8]` (`:277-290`).  So the
LABEL is a floor axis of the width guard under the JOIN clause (the selector flows into an lvalue the guarded body reads
and writes, and the switch re-tests it after the join).  A growth `len == 8 && relative_addr != CRIME_CONTROL` is the
canonical accept-row-failability mutant -- the joint guard-growth form -- and item 6 CLAIMS the accept side is pinned
"at any single cell".  Measured: C1, G1-G5 each 141/141, and G1/G2/G4/G5 change what the guest sees (an 8R of CONTROL
answers 0 not 0x100; an 8R of INTSTAT answers 0 with an interrupt pending; a 4W to REV from the window no longer
redirects; a 4W to INTMASK does not land).  All four of the seat's grounds hold on reading: the de-escalation clause
(pre-fix this edit ended the process at the first such access), the JOIN clause, item 6's own sentence, and the
`#499` shape (grok's `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0` was repaired at +0x00 by `acc8 near` and nowhere else).

**Why it is a class and not six cells:** `rearm once` and `latch x5` COUNT `-- declined.` (probe `:765`, `:949`).  A
wrongly declined accepted cell either comes after the first refusal (silenced) or IS the first refusal (takes the one
notice the lh +0x06 should have had).  Either way the count is 1.  So ACCEPTS carries the latch cross and carries nothing
about acceptance -- for any of its 46 cells.  Six seats read the enumeration and said CLOSES; none of the six engaged
with what the two ACCEPTS-fed rows can OBSERVE.  Counting arguments: one measured clean sweep outweighs seven readings
that did not look at the channel.

### R2 -- codex's row is the class closure for the ANNOUNCED form, and it is general by construction.

`row("latch said", said(t, 2, 0x06, clean), 1, ...)` in latch x5 (`said` at `:381` counts the exact announce text).  All
46 ACCEPTS accesses precede the lh +0x06 in that session (`:934-940`), so any guard growth at any of them sends that cell
into the announcing decline arm (`:314-319`) and the lh's own text is absent.  The window kinds are covered too: the
width arm declines BEFORE the pc test, so `swP/sdP/ldP/lwP` announce like any other (G4 measured red).  Base 149/149, red
on C1 and G1-G5, no shipped row moved, no command list changed.  **Take it as written; do not fold it into `latch x5`**
(a folded row loses the attribution of WHICH half failed).

### RA1 -- the SILENT bypass (G6).  (i) is REQUIRED; (ii) is LICENSED; the cell set is the ENUMERATION's or none.

G6 (`if (len == 8 && relative_addr == CRIME_CONTROL) goto crime_declined;` before the width test) is outside the
de-escalation class -- pre-fix an inserted `return 1;` was equally silent, so the deleted `exit(1)` never announced it --
and outside the DEPENDS-ON enumeration (it inserts a predicate; it weakens none).  So it is NOT floor, and no row can be
DEMANDED for it.  But item 6's literal "any single cell" is false of it, and a false record is L4 whatever the grade.

* **(i) REQUIRED:** narrow item 6 to "the ACCEPT SET shrunk at any single cell IN THE GUARD-GROWTH (announcing) FORM --
  carried by `latch said` over every ACCEPTS cell", and DISCLOSE the silent bypass BY CLASS, naming the cells where a
  value row catches it anyway (below) and the cells where it is EQUIVALENT at the guest (REV 8W/4W/4R out of the window,
  CONTROL 4R, TIME 4W/4R: the prelude rewrites or the high half is 0).
* **(ii) LICENSED (measured delta: `dg ld ctl`, `dg ld istat`, `dg sw imask`, `dg swP pc` each red on its cell, green on
  base, new sessions only).**  If value rows are added -- and I RECOMMEND it, because at those cells the silent form has
  the SAME guest effect as the growth form and a detector that sees one and not the other is claiming more than it
  pins -- the set is the Opus per-cell table's guest-visible "no" cells, all of them, in ONE diff: REV window 4W/8W/8R
  (the redirect), CONTROL 8R/8W, INTSTAT 8W/8R/4W, INTMASK 4W, TIME 8W, default `sw/lw 0x40`.  Twelve cells, each with
  a CHANNEL line in `PRETABLE.out` naming the mutant and the observable.  A five-cell set drawn from the mutants the
  seat happened to build is licensed too, but then the record says "5 of 12 rowed, 7 disclosed" -- it may not say the
  silent class is closed.  What I reject is only the half-set presented as closure.
* The coordinator's worry about incoherence is answered: (i) states what the CONTRACT claims; (ii) states what the
  DETECTOR additionally pins.  Different questions, both answered, no contradiction.

### RA2 -- repair 12 owes TARGETED TABLE 10 and CLOSING PASS 7.  The seat's augmentation run does not discharge either.

* **Table 10, and why the run is not it:** the seat APPENDED rows to a frozen copy; repair 12 re-types them into the
  shipped probe.  A re-typed row is a new instrument and is measured, not assumed.  Legs: base; C1, G1-G6, C2 (the new
  rows' own kills, including G6 for any value rows); the 17 table-9 pre-set kills IF POSTTABLE 10 will state their new
  scores (the "they also red `latch said`" prediction is REASONED -- the seat says so -- and a predicted score in a
  measured column is the frozen-number class; run them or omit them from the record, ~17 legs, about an hour in four
  lanes).  The 12 re-arm kills and every kill outside the two sessions cannot move: no command list changed (the
  targeting rule), and I checked that `latch said` reads latch x5's existing transcript only.
* **Pass 7 is owed** because the first VALID pass with zero floor breaches surviving adjudication is not pass 6.  It is
  LIGHT and it VERIFIES rather than hunts: (a) for each of the 46 ACCEPTS cells, name the row that reds under an
  ANNOUNCED decline (`latch said`, by construction) and under a SILENT decline (a value row, or "equivalent", or
  "disclosed") -- 92 cells, derivable by reading; (b) confirm by reading latch x5's command list that every ACCEPTS
  access precedes the lh (the generality argument); (c) nothing else.  Full roster, per the every-stage rule.

### RA3 -- is there a further accept-side class?  Yes, and it is the one pass 7 enumerates; plus one thing to BUILD.

* The accept side of the 46-cell cross has exactly two forms a single edit can produce: ANNOUNCED decline (closed by
  `latch said`) and SILENT decline (RA1).  That is the enumeration pass 7 verifies: cell x {announced, silent}.
* **A `d->warned`-keyed shrink must be BUILT before it is disclosed.**  The seat reasoned it clause (e) and did not build
  it.  Two forms: keyed on 8R (`len == 8 && (writeflag == MEM_WRITE || !(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN))`) -- I predict
  KILLED: `sb keeps`/`sh keeps`/`lo keeps`/`hi keeps`/`mid keeps` (`:691-842`) each read a seeded slot with an accepted ld
  AFTER a refusal, which such a shrink answers 0; and keyed on 8W -- may escape (the only accepted 8W after a refusal
  whose value is read back by an accepted read is unclear from the rows).  It is clause (e) by the DEPENDS-ON bound
  (`d->warned` is not a field the accept bodies read); if it escapes, DISCLOSE it by name.  "Reasoned, not built" is a
  prediction and this chain has had enough of those.
* Classes NOT to reopen: accepted-but-mishandled (landing, lane, re-map) are items 6/7's anchor rows and earlier tables'
  material, outside this chain's class.  Pass 7 is told so explicitly, or the chain does not terminate.

### A1-A9 dispositions
| # | ruling |
|---|---|

| A5 | Killed 139/141 both forms; premise true, conclusion refuted.  Upheld (none). |
| A6 | Killed 131/141; minimax's premise false (default-arm answer rows sit at unaligned offsets).  Upheld (none). |

| A9 | 40 floor + 2 clause-(e) strengthenings + 4 positional repeats = 46 (I recounted the tuple at `:731-743`).  deepseek's "six vs eight" is not an error: six KINDS, eight ENTRIES.  Upheld. |

### L4 wrong records in Part A (file: `regress/crime_width_probe.py` unless noted)
1. **Item 6, "the ACCEPT SET shrunk at any single cell"** -- false at every cell the Opus per-cell table marks "no".
2. **Item 3 (`:26-27`) "the arm's three TESTED idata bits (DOG 0x200, RESET 0x800, ENDIANESS 0x100)" and the comment at
   `:495` "ENDIANESS (0x0100) is the arm's third tested bit"** -- FALSE at both sites: `:375` is `idata &=
   ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;` with no `if`.  The header's own repair-11 note (`:728`) says the opposite correctly.  Two
   sites; the grep-for-siblings rule.
3. **DISCLOSED "ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots"** -- implies a touched slot is pinned; touching pins the LATCH, never the
   acceptance.
4. **DISCLOSED "an accepted WRITE at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away ... no round trip there can observe"** -- wrong twice: the
   window 4W redirects (G4 measured), and the 8-byte CONTROL write is unrowed (G1).

   for that access.  Name it.

## QUESTIONS

1. **Verify the accept-side enumeration.**  For each ACCEPTS cell, is the ANNOUNCED form (the width/accept guard grown by
   a conjunct, so the cell reaches the announcing decline) caught by `latch said', and is the SILENT form (a bypass that
   declines with no announce) either caught by a value row, EQUIVALENT (as item 6 names), or pinned by an existing row?
   Name any cell whose disposition is missing or wrong, and check the generality argument for `latch said' against the
   function (does every ACCEPTS access really run before the lh +0x06, and can a grown guard at some cell decline WITHOUT
   announcing while the latch is still clear?).
2. **Can each new row's channel vary under the mutant it names?**  In particular the IP2 rows (`istw sd`/`istw sw` -- the
   tail must not assert the line) and `acc time sd` (parity).
3. **Is there a floor gap in the 157-row test?**  The smallest edit to `dev_sgi_ip32.c` that undoes part of the fix's
   behaviour and still scores 157/157, as an exact edit, with the row you expected to catch it.  DISCLOSED items are
   outside the question; say if one is disclosed wrongly.
4. **Which statement in this brief, in POSTTABLE 10, or in the probe header, is wrong?**


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

  25: 
  26: * **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of eight passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
  27:   device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
  28:   tail, which restores HARDINT, recomputes INTSTAT (it does NOT call crime_interrupt_reassert, so the line is untouched),
  29:   answers a read 0 and returns 1.
  30: * **History**: 40 -> ... -> 141 -> 157 rows; TEN mutation-test tables, each with a written PRETABLE and a mechanical
  31:   prediction checker.  (The sixth-pass brief called the Opus fifth-closing seat's mutants "P1-P7"; table 9 ran four.)
  32: * **ACCEPTS** = 46 ENTRIES: the 40-cell pre-set/re-arm floor + 2 ENDIANESS strengthenings + 4 positional repeats.
  33: * **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).
  34: * **TIME** updates add 66 x elapsed microseconds -- always EVEN -- so an odd seed stays odd.
  35: 
  36: ## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)
  37: 
  38: ```c
  39: DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
  40: {
  41: 	/*
  42: 	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
  43: 	 *  physical 0x14000000.
  44: 	 *
  45: 	 *  On my real O2:
  46: 	 *
  47: 	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  48: 	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  49: 	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
  50: 	 *
  51: 	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
  52: 	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
  53: 	 *  would work too.)
  54: 	 *
  55: 	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
  56: 	 */
  57: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
  58: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
  59: 	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
  60: 	size_t i;
  61: 
  62: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
  63: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
  64: 
  65: 	/*
  66: 	 *  Set crime version/revision:
  67: 	 *
  68: 	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
  69: 	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
  70: 	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
  71: 	 *
  72: 	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
  73: 	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
  74: 	 */
  75: 	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
  76: 
  77: 	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
  78: 
  79: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
  80: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
  81: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
  82: 
  83: 	/*
  84: 	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
  85: 	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
  86: 	 *
  87: 	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
  88: 	 *  bank #0."
  89: 	 */
  90: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
  91: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
  92: 		exit(1);
  93: 	}
  94: 
  95: 	int mb_per_bank;
  96: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
  97: 		mb_per_bank = 32;
  98: 	else
  99: 		mb_per_bank = 128;
 100: 
 101: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
 102: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
 103: 			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
 104: 			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
 105: 		exit(1);
 106: 	}
 107: 
 108: 	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
 109: 	size_t total_mb = 0;
 110: 	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
 111: 		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
 112: 		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
 113: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
 114: 				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
 115: 		else
 116: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
 117: 
 118: 		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
 119: 	}
 120: 	
 121: 	if (len == 8) {
 122: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 123: 			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
 124: 		else
 125: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 126: 	} else if (len == 4) {
 127: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 128: 			if (relative_addr & 4) {
 129: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
 130: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
 131: 			} else {
 132: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
 133: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
 134: 			}
 135: 		} else {
 136: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 137: 			if (relative_addr & 4)
 138: 				odata = (int32_t)odata;
 139: 			else
 140: 				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
 141: 		}
 142: 	} else {
 143: 		/*
 144: 		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
 145: 		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
 146: 		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
 147: 		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
 148: 		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
 149: 		 *
 150: 		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
 151: 		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
 152: 		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
 153: 		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
 154: 		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
 155: 		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
 156: 		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
 157: 		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
 158: 		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
 159: 		 */
 160: 		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
 161: 			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
 162: 			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
 163: 			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
 164: 		}
 165: 		goto crime_declined;
 166: 	}
 167: 
 168: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 169: 
 170: 	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
 171: 		/*
 172: 		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
 173: 		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
 174: 		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
 175: 		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
 176: 		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
 177: 		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
 178: 		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
 179: 		 *
 180: 		 *  The address where this happens is:
 181: 		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
 182: 		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
 183: 		 *
 184: 		 *  4.18 works too.
 185: 		 *
 186: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
 187: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
 188: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
 189: 		 */
 190: 		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
 191: 			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
 192: 			if (cpu->is_32bit)
 193: 				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 194: 			else
 195: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 196: 		}
 197: 		break;
 198: 
 199: 	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
 200: 		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things

 succeeded in 188ms:
 201: 		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
 202: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 203: 			/*
 204: 			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
 205: 			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
 206: 			 */
 207: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
 208: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
 209: 			}
 210: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
 211: 				int j;
 212: 
 213: 				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
 214: 				    "reboot" command:  */
 215: 				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
 216: 					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
 217: 
 218: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
 219: 			}
 220: 
 221: 			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 222: 
 223: 			if (idata != 0)
 224: 				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
 225: 				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 226: 		}
 227: 		break;
 228: 
 229: 	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
 230: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
 231: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 232: 		break;
 233: 
 234: 	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
 235: 		// No warning.
 236: 		break;
 237: 
 238: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
 239: 		crime_update_crime_time(d);
 240: 		break;
 241: 
 242: 	default:
 243: 		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
 244: 			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
 245: 			    (int)relative_addr, len);
 246: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 247: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 248: 			debug(" ]\n");
 249: 		} else {
 250: 			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
 251: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 252: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 253: 			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
 254: 		}
 255: 	}
 256: 
 257:  crime_declined:
 258: 	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
 259: 	    and is still answered.  */
 260: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
 261: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 262: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 263: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 264: 
 265: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 266: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 267: 
 268: 	return 1;
 269: }
 270: ```

 451:     0x100', kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.  TOUCHING a slot in ACCEPTS pins its LATCH
 452:     behaviour, never its acceptance -- what pins acceptance is item 6 (`latch said' for the announced form, the value
 453:     rows for the silent form).  [This bullet implied a touched slot was pinned until repair 12 -- the Opus seat, closing
 454:     pass 6.]
 455:   * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
 456:     label) -- a refused store that lands only at
 457:     +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
 458:     +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
 459:     offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
 460:     keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
 461:     kind that can carry it (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; an aligned 2-byte access never sets
 462:     bit 0; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8 carried by no refusal at all; codex and the Opus seat,
 463:     fourth closing pass, found 2W LANDING at bit 1 set carried by no read-back store -- now +0x42, `lo keeps').  Note
 464:     `relative_addr / 8': a refused halfword at +0x1e would land on INTMASK's slot, not at +0x1e; the 2W session's
 465:     +0x1e store is not read back, and the INTMASK landing is carried by `imask keeps'.  A landing at
 466:     CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20, not a switch label) is in this class (deepseek, third closing pass).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
 467:     OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
 468:     which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
 469:     (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
 470:   * a ROUND TRIP cannot observe an accepted WRITE at +0x00 or +0x08 (the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL
 471:     on every access) -- but the write's EFFECTS can, and are rowed: from the PROM-probe window an accepted write
 472:     REDIRECTS the pc (revP sw pc, revP sd pc), and at CONTROL the arm's effects (sw08 halts, sw08 msg ctl at 4 bytes;
 473:     acc ctl sd at 8).  [This bullet said "no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted" as though
 474:     nothing could, and it was wrong twice: the window redirect (measured by the Opus seat, G4) and the 8-byte CONTROL
 475:     write, which no row carried until repair 12 -- closing pass 6.]  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc
 476:     ctl' (its redirect needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
 477:   * `odata = idata' in the decline arm: EQUIVALENT by construction -- `idata' is set only under MEM_WRITE and `odata' is
 478:     consumed only under MEM_READ (measured 141/141 on the 141-row test by the Opus seat, closing pass 6).  `cpu->is_32bit' (the REV arm's
 479:     pc-pointer refresh) holds one value per machine, like the configuration lvalues above.  Accepted-side address bits 7
 480:     and 8: the mid session's accepted sd +0x1f8 carries both, before and between its refusals (a latch keyed on them is
 481:     killed -- kimi-k3's C3, 139/141 on the 141-row test); an ACCEPT arm keyed on them is the unrowed-slot class above.
 482:   * ACCEPTS has 46 ENTRIES, not 46 kinds: the 40-cell pre-set/re-arm floor, the two ENDIANESS strengthenings, and four
 483:     positional repeats of default-arm kinds (`sw/lw 0x04' repeat 0x34's low lane; `sd/ld 0x278' repeat 0x40's 8-byte
 484:     kinds, and are the prologue's only accepted access with address bit 9 set -- an unrecorded strengthening until
 485:     repair 12; kimi CLI, closing pass 6).
 486:   * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME CONFINED to
 487:     bytes the model holds at 0 there -- REV = 0x11: bytes 1-7; CONTROL = 0x100: byte 0 and bytes 2-7; TIME: the 4-byte
 488:     half above the low word -- is EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV and CONTROL on every access; TIME is kept to 32
 489:     bits).  [Corrected 2026-09-14 from closing pass 5 (glm): this read "the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label)",
 490:     which at REV and CONTROL include a NONZERO byte -- item 9's v0 rows exist to catch exactly that selection.]  At
 491:     INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
 492:   * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
 493:     default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
 494:   * (closing pass 5; the Opus seat measured each at 141/141 on the 141-row test; the flagship ruling graded each clause
 495:     (e)) a latch re-armed or pre-set by a CONTROL write's ENDIANESS bit (0x100) -- the arm clears it unconditionally and
 496:     never branches on it; the two ENDIANESS writes in ACCEPTS since repair 11 are a strengthening, not a floor claim
 497:     over value bits; the announce CHANNEL keyed on a JOINT of axes (e.g. `(relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT && len == 2 ?
 498:     debug : fatal)(') -- item 5 rows the channel on each SINGLE axis; and a decline answering a MACHINE-CONFIGURATION
 499:     lvalue (`d->use_fb', `flag_for_128MB'), which holds one value per machine and is 0 on this probe's -M 64 non-graphics
 500:     ip32 -- a graphics O2 (use_fb = 1) would see `odata = d->use_fb' answer 1.  Unrowable on one machine, like the
 501:     function-static latch below.
 502:   * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
 503:     CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
 504:   * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
 505:     (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
 506:   * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
 507:     process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
 508:     recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
 509:   * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
 510:     not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
 511:   * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).

 679: +row("free revP said", ran.count("unimplemented len=1 at 0x0 -- declined.  (once) ]") if clean else "unclean", 1,
 680: +    "free-running lb +0x00 FROM the PROM window")
 681: +
 682: +#  THE SILENT FORM (repair 12, licensed): an accepted cell bypassed with NO announce -- `if (len == 8 && relative_addr ==
 683: +#  CRIME_CONTROL) goto crime_declined;' before the width test (the Opus seat's G6, 141/141 with `latch said' green) --
 684: +#  is outside the de-escalation class (the deleted exit(1) would not have announced it either) and is DISCLOSED by class
 685: +#  in item 6; these VALUE rows carry the cells where such a bypass is GUEST-VISIBLE, derived per cell from the switch and
 686: +#  the width arm (the flagship ruling: from the enumeration, never from the mutants that happened to be built):
 687: +#    REV window 4W/8W/8R, CONTROL 8R/8W, INTSTAT 8W/8R/4W, INTMASK 4W, TIME 8W, the default arm's 4W/4R at +0x40.
 688: +#  The other ACCEPTS cells are either pinned elsewhere or EQUIVALENT when bypassed (item 6).
 689: +#  REV window: CHANNEL = the PROM-probe redirect (pc = ra), which only an ACCEPTED access reaches.
 690: +A = Addr(CODE)
 691: +c = ["ra=0x%x" % RA]
 692: +x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0, 0, at=PROBE_PC); c += x + ["print pc"]
 693: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0, 0, at=PROBE_PC); c += x + ["print pc"]
 694: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0, "s0", at=PROBE_PC); c += x + ["print pc"]
 695: +t, clean = session(c)
 696: +for i, k in enumerate(("sw", "sd", "ld")):
 697: +    v = nth(t, "pc", i)
 698: +    row("revP %s pc" % k, "absent" if v is None else "0x%x" % v, "0x%x" % RA,
 699: +        "accepted %s CRIME_REV from 0xbfc05180: pc = ra" % k)
 700: +#  CONTROL 8R: CHANNEL = its value, 0x100 (the prelude's ENDIANESS; a declined read answers 0).  INTSTAT 8R: its value
 701: +#  SOFTINT | HARDINT, recomputed by every access's tail.  INTMASK 4W: the high half lands (ld +0x18).  TIME 8W: an ODD
 702: +#  seed stays odd (every update adds a multiple of 66), and the register is even before it -- so parity says whether
 703: +#  the store landed, whatever the host clock added.  Default arm +0x40 4W (the high lane) and 4R: the value round trip.
 704: +#  CONTROL 8W: its arm prints the unimplemented-control text for a leftover bit (0x01), which a declined store cannot.
 705: +A = Addr(CODE)
 706: +c = []
 707: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x08, "s0"); c += x
 708: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
 709: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x10, "s1"); c += x
 710: +x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x18, 0x10000000); c += x
 711: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x18, "s2"); c += x
 712: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x38, 0x11111111); c += x
 713: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x38, "s3"); c += x
 714: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x40, 0); c += x
 715: +x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x40, 0x0a0b0c0d); c += x
 716: +x, _ = ld(A, "lw", CRIME + 0x40, "a1"); c += x
 717: +x, _ = ld(A, "ld", CRIME + 0x40, "a2"); c += x
 718: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x08, 0x01); c += x
 719: +t, clean = session(c + ["print s0", "print s1", "print s2", "print s3", "print a1", "print a2"])
 720: +tv = nth(t, "s3", 0)
 721: +row("acc ctl ld", pr(t, "s0"), "0x100", "accepted ld +0x08 answers CONTROL (0x100)")
 722: +row("acc istat ld", pr(t, "s1"), "0x%x" % BIT, "accepted ld +0x10 answers INTSTAT = SOFTINT")
 723: +row("acc imask sw", pr(t, "s2"), "0x1000000000000000", "accepted sw +0x18 lands in the high half")
 724: +row("acc time sd", "absent" if tv is None else ("odd" if tv & 1 else "even"), "odd",
 725: +    "accepted sd +0x38 of an odd seed: TIME stays odd (raw %s)" % tv)
 726: +row("acc 40 lw", pr(t, "a1"), "0xa0b0c0d", "accepted lw +0x40 answers the high half the sw stored")
 727: +row("acc 40 sw", pr(t, "a2"), "0xa0b0c0d00000000", "accepted sw +0x40 lands in the high half (ld)")
 728: +row("acc ctl sd", count(t, "CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented", clean), 1, "accepted sd 0x01 +0x08 prints the text")
 729: +#  INTSTAT 8W and 4W: CHANNEL = cause.IP2.  With INTMASK = SOFTINT = BIT and the line low (SOFTINT's store goes to the
 730: +#  default arm, which does not reassert), an ACCEPTED store at +0x10 reasserts and raises IP2; a declined one reaches the
 731: +#  tail, which recomputes INTSTAT but does NOT touch the line.  Between the two, SOFTINT = 0 and an INTMASK store lower it.
 732: +A = Addr(CODE)
 733: +c = []
 734: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
 735: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
 736: +c += ["print cause"]
 737: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x10, 0); c += x
 738: +c += ["print cause"]
 739: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, 0); c += x
 740: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x18, BIT); c += x
 741: +x, _ = st(A, "sd", CRIME + 0x20, BIT); c += x
 742: +c += ["print cause"]                                # immediately before the sw, as `pre' is before the sd
 743: +x, _ = st(A, "sw", CRIME + 0x10, 0); c += x
 744: +c += ["print cause"]
 745: +t, clean = session(c)
 746: +row("istw pre", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 0)), "clear", "CONTROL: IP2 low after SOFTINT's default-arm store")
 747: +row("istw sd", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 1)), "set", "accepted sd +0x10 reasserts: IP2 set")
 748: +row("istw mid", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 2)), "clear", "CONTROL: lowered again (SOFTINT 0, INTMASK store), SOFTINT set")
 749: +row("istw sw", ip2(nth(t, "cause", 3)), "set", "accepted sw +0x10 reasserts: IP2 set")
 750:  
 751:  npass = sum(1 for _, ok in rows if ok)
 752: ```

 756: ## PART A -- #529 crimewidthkill, closing pass 6, repair 11, 141 rows
 757: 
 758: ### R1 -- A1 is a FLOOR breach, L2, and it is a CLASS.  Verified against the source, not taken from the seat.
 759: 
 760: The width arm (`dev_sgi_ip32.c:275-296`) branches on `len == 8`, `len == 4`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr & 4`; every
 761: accepted access then reaches `switch (relative_addr)` (`:322`) and `d->reg[relative_addr / 8]` (`:277-290`).  So the
 762: LABEL is a floor axis of the width guard under the JOIN clause (the selector flows into an lvalue the guarded body reads
 763: and writes, and the switch re-tests it after the join).  A growth `len == 8 && relative_addr != CRIME_CONTROL` is the
 764: canonical accept-row-failability mutant -- the joint guard-growth form -- and item 6 CLAIMS the accept side is pinned
 765: "at any single cell".  Measured: C1, G1-G5 each 141/141, and G1/G2/G4/G5 change what the guest sees (an 8R of CONTROL
 766: answers 0 not 0x100; an 8R of INTSTAT answers 0 with an interrupt pending; a 4W to REV from the window no longer
 767: redirects; a 4W to INTMASK does not land).  All four of the seat's grounds hold on reading: the de-escalation clause
 768: (pre-fix this edit ended the process at the first such access), the JOIN clause, item 6's own sentence, and the
 769: `#499` shape (grok's `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0` was repaired at +0x00 by `acc8 near` and nowhere else).
 770: 
 771: **Why it is a class and not six cells:** `rearm once` and `latch x5` COUNT `-- declined.` (probe `:765`, `:949`).  A
 772: wrongly declined accepted cell either comes after the first refusal (silenced) or IS the first refusal (takes the one
 773: notice the lh +0x06 should have had).  Either way the count is 1.  So ACCEPTS carries the latch cross and carries nothing
 774: about acceptance -- for any of its 46 cells.  Six seats read the enumeration and said CLOSES; none of the six engaged
 775: with what the two ACCEPTS-fed rows can OBSERVE.  Counting arguments: one measured clean sweep outweighs seven readings
 776: that did not look at the channel.
 777:

 779: 
 780: `row("latch said", said(t, 2, 0x06, clean), 1, ...)` in latch x5 (`said` at `:381` counts the exact announce text).  All
 781: 46 ACCEPTS accesses precede the lh +0x06 in that session (`:934-940`), so any guard growth at any of them sends that cell
 782: into the announcing decline arm (`:314-319`) and the lh's own text is absent.  The window kinds are covered too: the
 783: width arm declines BEFORE the pc test, so `swP/sdP/ldP/lwP` announce like any other (G4 measured red).  Base 149/149, red
 784: on C1 and G1-G5, no shipped row moved, no command list changed.  **Take it as written; do not fold it into `latch x5`**
 785: (a folded row loses the attribution of WHICH half failed).

 788:

 792: DEMANDED for it.  But item 6's literal "any single cell" is false of it, and a false record is L4 whatever the grade.

 799:   base, new sessions only).**  If value rows are added -- and I RECOMMEND it, because at those cells the silent form has
 800:   the SAME guest effect as the growth form and a detector that sees one and not the other is claiming more than it

 succeeded in 168ms:
 801:   pins -- the set is the Opus per-cell table's guest-visible "no" cells, all of them, in ONE diff: REV window 4W/8W/8R

 754: ## THE FLAGSHIP RULING, PART A (closing pass 6)
 755: 
 756: ## PART A -- #529 crimewidthkill, closing pass 6, repair 11, 141 rows
 757: 
 758: ### R1 -- A1 is a FLOOR breach, L2, and it is a CLASS.  Verified against the source, not taken from the seat.
 759: 
 760: The width arm (`dev_sgi_ip32.c:275-296`) branches on `len == 8`, `len == 4`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr & 4`; every
 761: accepted access then reaches `switch (relative_addr)` (`:322`) and `d->reg[relative_addr / 8]` (`:277-290`).  So the
 762: LABEL is a floor axis of the width guard under the JOIN clause (the selector flows into an lvalue the guarded body reads
 763: and writes, and the switch re-tests it after the join).  A growth `len == 8 && relative_addr != CRIME_CONTROL` is the
 764: canonical accept-row-failability mutant -- the joint guard-growth form -- and item 6 CLAIMS the accept side is pinned
 765: "at any single cell".  Measured: C1, G1-G5 each 141/141, and G1/G2/G4/G5 change what the guest sees (an 8R of CONTROL
 766: answers 0 not 0x100; an 8R of INTSTAT answers 0 with an interrupt pending; a 4W to REV from the window no longer
 767: redirects; a 4W to INTMASK does not land).  All four of the seat's grounds hold on reading: the de-escalation clause
 768: (pre-fix this edit ended the process at the first such access), the JOIN clause, item 6's own sentence, and the
 769: `#499` shape (grok's `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0` was repaired at +0x00 by `acc8 near` and nowhere else).
 770: 
 771: **Why it is a class and not six cells:** `rearm once` and `latch x5` COUNT `-- declined.` (probe `:765`, `:949`).  A
 772: wrongly declined accepted cell either comes after the first refusal (silenced) or IS the first refusal (takes the one
 773: notice the lh +0x06 should have had).  Either way the count is 1.  So ACCEPTS carries the latch cross and carries nothing
 774: about acceptance -- for any of its 46 cells.  Six seats read the enumeration and said CLOSES; none of the six engaged
 775: with what the two ACCEPTS-fed rows can OBSERVE.  Counting arguments: one measured clean sweep outweighs seven readings
 776: that did not look at the channel.
 777: 
 778: ### R2 -- codex's row is the class closure for the ANNOUNCED form, and it is general by construction.
 779:

  68: #define	CRIME_TICKSHIFT			14
  69: 
  70: 
  71: struct macepci_data {
  72: 	struct pci_data *pci_data;
  73: };
  74: 
  75: #define	DEV_CRIME_LENGTH		0x280
  76: struct crime_data {
  77: 	uint64_t		reg[DEV_CRIME_LENGTH / sizeof(uint64_t)];
  78: 
  79: 	uint64_t		last_microseconds;
  80: 
  81: 	struct interrupt	irq;
  82: 	int			prev_asserted;
  83: 
  84: 	int			use_fb;
  85: 
  86: 	/*  #529: warn-once latches for guest-drivable diagnostics, one bit
  87: 	    per site and never shared.  memset(0) at init clears them.  */
  88: 	int			warned;
  89: };
  90: 
  91: #define	CRIME_W_LEN		(1 << 0)	/*  #529: the width decline  */
  92: 
  93: 
  94: void mips_pc_to_pointers(struct cpu *);
  95: void mips32_pc_to_pointers(struct cpu *);
  96: 
  97: 
  98: void crime_interrupt_reassert(struct crime_data *d)
  99: {
 100: 	uint64_t status =
 101: 		d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 102: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 103: 
 104: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = status;
 105: 
 106: 	status &= d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 107: 
 108: #if 0
 109: 	printf("CRIME SOFTINT=0x%08x HARDINT=0x%08x => 0x%08x, INTMASK=0x%08x\n",
 110: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 111: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 112: 		(uint32_t)status,
 113: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 114: #endif
 115: 	
 116: 	int asserted = !!status;
 117: 
 118: 	if (asserted && !d->prev_asserted)
 119: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 120: 	else if (!asserted && d->prev_asserted)
 121: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 122: 	
 123: 	d->prev_asserted = asserted;
 124: }
 125: 
 126: /*
 127:  *  crime_interrupt_assert():
 128:  *  crime_interrupt_deassert():
 129:  */
 130: void crime_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 131: {
 132: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 133: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |= interrupt->line;
 134: 	//printf("CRIME asserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 135: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 136: }
 137: void crime_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 138: {
 139: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 140: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] &= ~interrupt->line;
 141: 	//printf("CRIME deasserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 142: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 143: }
 144: 
 145: 
 146: void crime_update_crime_time(struct crime_data* d)
 147: {
 148: 	struct timeval tv;
 149: 	
 150: 	gettimeofday(&tv, NULL);
 151: 
 152: 	uint64_t microseconds = tv.tv_sec * 1000000 + tv.tv_usec;
 153: 	if (d->last_microseconds == 0)
 154: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 155: 
 156: 	int64_t delta = microseconds - d->last_microseconds;
 157: 
 158: 	if (delta < 0) {
 159: 		fatal("[ crime_update_crime_time: host system time went backwards? ]\n");
 160: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 161: 		delta = 0;
 162: 	}
 163: 
 164: 	// The delta to add is 66 per microsecond.
 165: 	int64_t to_add = delta * 66;
 166: 
 167: 	if (to_add >= 1) {
 168: 		// NetBSD says CRIME_TIME_MASK = 0x0000ffffffffffffULL
 169: 		// but my O2 seems to use only the lower 32 bits as an
 170: 		// _unsigned_ value. (TODO: Double-check this again.)
 171: 		d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 172: 			(uint32_t)(d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] + to_add);
 173: 
 174: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 175: 	}
 176: }
 177: 
 178: 
 179: /*
 180:  *  dev_crime_tick():
 181:  *
 182:  *  Updates CRIME_TIME (at 66 MHz) and reassert CRIME interrupts.
 183:  */
 184: DEVICE_TICK(crime)
 185: {
 186: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
 187: 
 188: 	crime_update_crime_time(d);
 189: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 190: }
 191: 
 192: 
 193: DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
 194: {
 195: 	/*
 196: 	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
 197: 	 *  physical 0x14000000.
 198: 	 *
 199: 	 *  On my real O2:
 200: 	 *
 201: 	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 202: 	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 203: 	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 204: 	 *
 205: 	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
 206: 	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
 207: 	 *  would work too.)
 208: 	 *
 209: 	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
 210: 	 */
 211: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
 212: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 213: 	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 214: 	size_t i;
 215: 
 216: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 217: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 218: 
 219: 	/*
 220: 	 *  Set crime version/revision:
 221: 	 *
 222: 	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
 223: 	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
 224: 	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
 225: 	 *
 226: 	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
 227: 	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
 228: 	 */
 229: 	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
 230: 
 231: 	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 232: 
 233: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 234: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 235: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 236: 
 237: 	/*
 238: 	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
 239: 	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
 240: 	 *
 241: 	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
 242: 	 *  bank #0."
 243: 	 */
 244: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
 245: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
 246: 		exit(1);
 247: 	}
 248: 
 249: 	int mb_per_bank;
 250: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
 251: 		mb_per_bank = 32;
 252: 	else
 253: 		mb_per_bank = 128;
 254: 
 255: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
 256: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
 257: 			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
 258: 			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
 259: 		exit(1);
 260: 	}
 261: 
 262: 	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
 263: 	size_t total_mb = 0;
 264: 	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
 265: 		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
 266: 		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
 267: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
 268: 				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
 269: 		else
 270: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
 271: 
 272: 		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
 273: 	}
 274: 	
 275: 	if (len == 8) {
 276: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 277: 			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
 278: 		else
 279: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 280: 	} else if (len == 4) {
 281: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 282: 			if (relative_addr & 4) {
 283: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
 284: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
 285: 			} else {
 286: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
 287: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
 288: 			}
 289: 		} else {
 290: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 291: 			if (relative_addr & 4)
 292: 				odata = (int32_t)odata;
 293: 			else
 294: 				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
 295: 		}
 296: 	} else {
 297: 		/*
 298: 		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
 299: 		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
 300: 		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
 301: 		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
 302: 		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
 303: 		 *
 304: 		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
 305: 		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
 306: 		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
 307: 		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
 308: 		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
 309: 		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
 310: 		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
 311: 		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
 312: 		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
 313: 		 */
 314: 		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
 315: 			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
 316: 			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
 317: 			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
 318: 		}
 319: 		goto crime_declined;
 320: 	}
 321: 
 322: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 323: 
 324: 	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
 325: 		/*
 326: 		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
 327: 		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
 328: 		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
 329: 		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
 330: 		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
 331: 		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
 332: 		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
 333: 		 *
 334: 		 *  The address where this happens is:
 335: 		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
 336: 		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
 337: 		 *
 338: 		 *  4.18 works too.
 339: 		 *
 340: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
 341: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
 342: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
 343: 		 */
 344: 		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
 345: 			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
 346: 			if (cpu->is_32bit)
 347: 				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 348: 			else
 349: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 350: 		}
 351: 		break;
 352: 
 353: 	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
 354: 		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
 355: 		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
 356: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 357: 			/*
 358: 			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
 359: 			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
 360: 			 */
 361: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
 362: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
 363: 			}
 364: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
 365: 				int j;
 366: 
 367: 				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
 368: 				    "reboot" command:  */
 369: 				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
 370: 					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
 371: 
 372: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
 373: 			}
 374: 
 375: 			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 376: 
 377: 			if (idata != 0)
 378: 				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
 379: 				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 380: 		}
 381: 		break;
 382: 
 383: 	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
 384: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
 385: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 386: 		break;
 387: 
 388: 	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
 389: 		// No warning.
 390: 		break;
 391: 
 392: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
 393: 		crime_update_crime_time(d);
 394: 		break;
 395: 
 396: 	default:
 397: 		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
 398: 			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
 399: 			    (int)relative_addr, len);
 400: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 401: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 402: 			debug(" ]\n");
 403: 		} else {
 404: 			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
 405: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 406: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 407: 			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
 408: 		}
 409: 	}
 410: 
 411:  crime_declined:
 412: 	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
 413: 	    and is still answered.  */
 414: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
 415: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 416: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 417: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 418: 
 419: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 420: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 421: 
 422: 	return 1;
 423: }
 424: 
 425: 
 426: /*
 427:  *  dev_crime_init():
 428:  */
 429: void dev_crime_init(struct machine *machine, struct memory *mem,
 430: 	uint64_t baseaddr, char *irq_path, int use_fb)
 431: {
 432: 	struct crime_data *d;
 433: 	char tmpstr[200];
 434: 	int i;
 435: 
 436: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct crime_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct crime_data)));
 437: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct crime_data));
 438: 
 439: 	d->use_fb = use_fb;
 440: 
 441: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(irq_path, d->irq);
 442: 
 443: 	/*  Register 32 crime interrupts (hexadecimal names):  */
 444: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
 445: 		struct interrupt templ;

   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: """
   3: crime_width_probe.py BIN [LABEL] -- #529 crimewidthkill DETECTOR, rung 3, cold debugger, UNMODIFIED `-E sgi -e ip32'.
   4: 
   5: src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c, DEVICE_ACCESS(crime): an access whose length is neither 8 nor 4 used to reach
   6: 
   7:         fatal("crime access len = %i!\n", len);
   8:         exit(1);
   9: 
  10: so one guest lb/lbu/lh/lhu/sb/sh anywhere in CRIME (physical 0x14000000, KSEG1 0xb4000000) ended the emulator
  11: (witnessed for all six widths at rung 3: regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/crime_width_witness_20260913.py).  #529 DECLINES the width
  12: with one latched `(once)' announce and a `goto' to the function's common tail, skipping the register switch -- the
  13: #498 sgi_de shape.  A read is answered 0 by the tail, a write is dropped, the PC advances, no exception is raised.
  14: 
  15: Every access here is a real MIPS guest instruction through real address decode and memory_rw, one `step' at a time.
  16: 
  17: CONTRACT -- the edits this detector is designed to catch (clause (e)), each MEASURED in a kill table under
  18: artifacts/crimewidthkill/ (killtable_20260913/ through killtable10_20260915/):
  19:   1. the kill reinstated verbatim, or any other host death at the width arm, before or after the announce
 253:   * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).
 254: 
 255: Sentinel: CRIMEP_DONE (plus CRIMEP_RESULT=n/N and CRIMEP_CONTROL=OK|FAIL).
 256: """
 257: import os, pty, re, select, signal, sys, time
 258: 
 259: BIN = sys.argv[1]
 260: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
 261: DUMMY = "/tmp/dummy_crimep.bin"
 262: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
 263:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
 264:         f.write(b"\0" * 16)
 265: 
 266: CODE = 0x80001000
 267: SCRATCH_RAM = 0xa0001800
 268: LIVE_VAL = 0x12345678
 269: CRIME = 0xb4000000
 270: SENT = 0xdeadbeef
 271: RA = 0x80004000
 272: PROBE_PC = 0xbfc05180           # inside the CRIME_REV hack's window, (pc & 0xffffff00) == 0xbfc05100
 273: 
 274: R = {"zero": 0, "at": 1, "v0": 2, "v1": 3, "a0": 4, "a1": 5, "a2": 6, "a3": 7, "t0": 8, "t1": 9,
 275:      "s0": 16, "s1": 17, "s2": 18, "s3": 19, "ra": 31}
 276: OPC = {"lb": 0x20, "lh": 0x21, "lw": 0x23, "lbu": 0x24, "lhu": 0x25, "ld": 0x37,
 277:        "sb": 0x28, "sh": 0x29, "sw": 0x2b, "sd": 0x3f}
 278: OP_B_SELF = 0x1000ffff
 279: OP_NOP = 0
 280: 
 281: 
 282: def enc(op, rs, rt, imm=0):
 283:     return (OPC[op] << 26) | (R[rs] << 21) | (R[rt] << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)
 284: 
 285: 
 286: #  hand-assembled encodings checked against the committed witness's constants (a wrong register field reads 0, which
 287: #  a decline's own answer would accept by accident -- so the accept rows carry nonzero wants)
 288: assert enc("lb", "at", "v0") == 0x80220000 and enc("lhu", "at", "v0") == 0x94220000
 289: assert enc("sb", "a3", "t0") == 0xa0e80000 and enc("sh", "a3", "t0") == 0xa4e80000
 290: assert enc("sw", "a3", "t0") == 0xace80000 and enc("lw", "at", "v0") == 0x8c220000
 291: 
 292: rows = []
 293: 
 294: 
 295: def row(name, got, want, note=""):
 296:     ok = (got == want)
 297:     rows.append((name, ok))
 298:     print("%-16s %-20s %-20s %-40s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
 299:     sys.stdout.flush()
 300: 
 301: 
 302: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
 303:     """one emulator, read to the pty's EOF after `quit'.  Returns (text, clean): clean = EOF reached AND exit 0."""
 304:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 305:     if pid == 0:
 306:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64", "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
 307:         os._exit(127)
 308:     buf = ""
 309: 
 310:     def rd(t):
 311:         nonlocal buf
 312:         end = time.time() + t
 313:         while time.time() < end:
 314:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 315:             if not r:
 316:                 continue
 317:             try:
 318:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 319:             except OSError:
 320:                 return False
 321:             if not d:
 322:                 return False
 323:             buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 324:         return True
 325: 
 326:     rd(settle)
 327:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 328:         try:
 329:             if c == "<RUN>":        # free-run: `continue', let the guest run, then break back in with CTRL-C
 330:                 os.write(fd, b"continue\n")
 331:                 ok = rd(2.0)
 332:                 os.write(fd, b"\x03")
 333:                 ok = ok and rd(1.5)
 334:             else:
 335:                 os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 336:                 ok = rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25)
 337:         except OSError:
 338:             break
 339:         if not ok:
 340:             break
 341:     eof = True                      # a process already gone before `quit' has closed the pty; its status says how
 342:     try:
 343:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
 344:         eof = not rd(10.0)          # to EOF, so anything printed on the way out is in the transcript
 345:     except OSError:
 346:         pass
 347:     if not eof:
 348:         try:
 349:             os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)    # a session that never reached EOF is ended, never waited on forever
 350:         except OSError:
 351:             pass
 352:     try:
 353:         os.close(fd)
 354:     except OSError:
 355:         pass
 356:     how = "nostatus"
 357:     try:
 358:         _, stat = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
 359:         how = ("sig%d" % os.WTERMSIG(stat)) if os.WIFSIGNALED(stat) else ("exit%d" % os.WEXITSTATUS(stat))
 360:     except OSError:
 361:         pass
 362:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), (eof and how == "exit0")
 363: 
 364: 
 365: def pr(t, reg):
 366:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 367:     return ("0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)) if m else "absent"
 368: 
 369: 
 370: def exc(t):
 371:     c = pr(t, "cause")
 372:     return ("%d" % ((int(c, 16) >> 2) & 0x1f)) if c != "absent" else "absent"
 373: 
 374: 
 375: def count(t, s, clean):
 376:     """a text count is evidence only from a whole transcript: an unclean session reads `unclean', never a number"""
 377:     return t.count(s) if clean else "unclean"
 378: 
 379: 
 380: class Addr(object):
 381:     def __init__(self, base):
 382:         self.a = base
 383: 
 384:     def take(self, n):
 385:         got = self.a
 386:         self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20
 387:         return got

 390: def run_ops(ops, at):

 532: e, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x08, 0x0900)       # glm, closing pass: ENDIANESS (0x0100) -- CLEARED by the arm, never
 533:                                                # branched on (this said "the arm's third tested bit" until repair 12)
 534: y, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x08, 0x0a00)
 535: f, _ = st(A, "sh", CRIME + 0x08, 0xffff)        # all-ones: every bit a data-keyed narrowing could key on, set
 536: #  and a follow-up refusal at ANOTHER label (the codex seat, second closing pass: a latch whose bit is keyed on one label,
 537: #  `CRIME_W_LEN << (relative_addr == L)', is invisible to a session that refuses only at L)
 538: g, _ = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "t1")
 539: t, clean = session(x + e + y + f + AFTER + roundtrip(A) + g)
 540: row("sh08b msg", count(t, "CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented", clean), 0, "2W sh 0x0201 +0x08 (DOG set): no CONTROL text")
 541: row("sh08b run", pr(t, "v1"), "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL, "2W sh 0x0900, 0x0a00 +0x08 (E/DOG+RESET): CPUs NOT stopped")
 542: row("sh08b once", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "4 refusals at CONTROL, then +0x07: one notice")
 543: #  the FIRST refusal's own text (codex, third closing pass: a notice suppressed at one label -- or at one address-bit
 544: #  value -- and printed instead by the +0x07 follow-up still counts one; every said row sat at an offset with bit 1 set)
 545: row("sh08b said", said(t, 2, 0x8, clean), 1, "sh +0x08 (first refusal): its own announce")
 546: 
 547: for op in ("lb", "lh"):
 548:     A = Addr(CODE)
 549:     x, _ = ld(A, op, CRIME + 0, "v0", at=PROBE_PC)
 550:     more = ld(A, "lb", CRIME + 0x07, "v1")[0] if op == "lb" else []     # a second refusal: the REV-first latch cell
 551:     t, clean = session(["ra=0x%x" % RA] + x + AFTER + ["print v0"] + more)
 552:     row("%s rev pc" % op, pr(t, "pc"), "0x%x" % (PROBE_PC + 4), "%dR at CRIME_REV from 0xbfc05180: pc NOT ra"
 553:         % (1 if op == "lb" else 2))

 555:     if op == "lb":

 564:     row("%s rev pc" % op, pr(t, "pc"), "0x%x" % (PROBE_PC + 4), "%dW at CRIME_REV from 0xbfc05180: pc NOT ra"
 565:         % (1 if op == "sb" else 2))
 566: 
 567: # ---- the INTSTAT / INTMASK labels: their arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), which is NOT idempotent -- ----
 568: #  INTMASK = bit 28 and SOFTINT = bit 28 are stored with 8-byte writes; SOFTINT's store goes to the default arm and does
 569: #  NOT reassert, so the CPU's IP2 (cause bit 0x400) stays low until something reasserts.  A refused access at the label
 570: #  must leave it low; the accepted `lw' that follows is the CONTROL that the arm reasserts at once.  (Measured by the
 571: #  pass-2 Opus seat, opus_pass2_20260913/scripts/crime_p2_escape.py E1/E2: a fall-through at the label sets IP2.)
 572: #  BIT carries bit 0 as well (the kimi CLI seat, second closing pass): a refused read answers the low bytes of whatever
 573: #  the decline hands the tail, so with a register whose low bytes were 0 a decline that answered THE REGISTER
 574: #  (`odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' at INTSTAT or INTMASK) read back 0 and passed.  AND in its TOP lanes (the Opus
 575: #  second-closing seat, measured 121/121 on the 0x10000001 seed): a decline that answers the bytes the ADDRESS names --
 576: #  `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))', the lane the model's own 4-byte read
 577: #  path uses -- reads bytes 0-1 at the label, which the 0x10000001 seed left 0.  So the seed is nonzero in byte 0, byte 1
 578: #  and the low byte; INTSTAT = SOFTINT | HARDINT and INTMASK both carry it; the reassert still sees bit 28.
 579: #  And glm (third closing pass) found a THIRD byte selection -- the 4-byte path's half, `reg >> 32' -- whose low bytes
 580: #  that seed left 0.  So the seed is now nonzero in EVERY byte: no selection of the register's bytes can answer 0.  (DOG,
 581: #  the far slot and the middle slot are seeded every-byte-nonzero already.)
 582: BIT = 0x0101010111010101
 583: 
 584: 
 585: def nth(t, reg, n):
 586:     ms = re.findall(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 587:     return int(ms[n], 16) if len(ms) > n else None
 588: 
 589: 
 590: def ip2(v):
 591:     return "absent" if v is None else ("set" if v & 0x400 else "clear")
 592: 
 593: 
 594: for lbl, off in (("imask", 0x18), ("istat", 0x10)):

 609:     c += ["print cause", "print a0", "print a1"]

 622:     row("%s ctl" % lbl, ip2(nth(t, "cause", 5)), "set", "CONTROL: accepted lw +0x%x reasserts" % off)
 623:     row("%s once" % lbl, count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "4 refusals at +0x%x, then +0x07: one notice" % off)
 624:     row("%s said" % lbl, said(t, 1, off, clean), 1, "lb +0x%x (first refusal): its own announce" % off)
 625:     row("%s lb v0" % lbl, pr(t, "a0"), "0x0", "refused lb +0x%x answers 0 (register 0x0101..01)" % off)

 629: 
 630: # ---- THE DECLINE HOLDS WHILE AN INTERRUPT IS LATCHED (the Opus closing seat: `if (!d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8]) goto
 631: #  crime_declined;' passed the 85-row version -- every refusal in it ran with HARDINT == 0, and the tail the goto jumps
 632: #  to READS HARDINT, so the field is a DEPENDS-ON operand; `if (!d->prev_asserted)' passed too).  The guest latches a
 633: #  hardware interrupt by two MACE stores (ISA interrupt status, then mask -> crime_interrupt_assert -> HARDINT), and

 635: #  asserts the CRIME line (INTMASK and SOFTINT = BIT, reasserted by an accepted lw), then a refused sh 0x0800 at

 663: # ---- the TIME label: an accepted read returns the value of the PREVIOUS update, then updates -- so a refused access
 664: #  that reached crime_update_crime_time() would show in the NEXT read.  The first `ld' latches last_microseconds; on the
 665: #  fixed build the only other update before the next `ld' is the device tick's FIRST firing -- ticks_till_next starts at
 666: #  0, so it fires right after the session's first `ld' (the Opus closing seat; this said "nothing else updates TIME"
 667: #  until then), and it adds the few microseconds since the latch: the 66-990 the rows print (measured across 52 cells
 668: #  against SMALL = 6.6e6).  Later ticks are 2^14 cycles apart and a session steps a few dozen instructions; a fall-through at 0x38
 669: #  makes it the wall-clock interval of the intervening debugger commands, ~1e8.  The third `ld' is the CONTROL that the
 670: #  channel moves.  (Opus pass 2, E3; the tick-spacing wording corrected by the kimi CLI seat on the F2 pass.)
 671: #  ASSUMPTION, disclosed: after that FIRST tick, no further tick falls between the first `ld' and the `ld' that samples

 674: #  dev_crime_tick also calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), so a tick inside a session could raise IP2 on the fixed build
